2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Identify the cars that are currently available in the store, sorted by daily price.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize an empty list 'availableCars'.
2. For each car in this.cars:
   a. Check if there exists any rental in this.rentals where rental.car == car and rental.backDate is null.
   b. If no such rental exists, add the car to 'availableCars'.
3. Sort 'availableCars' by dailyPrice in ascending order.
4. Return 'availableCars'.
Related Features: cars, rentals
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car>: A list of available cars sorted by daily price, or an empty list if none are available.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned list contains only cars not tied to active rentals (backDate is null), sorted by price.

Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize totalRevenue = 0.0.
2. For each rental in this.rentals:
   a. Add rental.totalPrice to totalRevenue.
3. Return totalRevenue.
Related Features: rentals
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: double: The sum of total prices from all rental records.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned value is the sum of all rental total prices.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store.
Algorithm:
1. If this.cars is empty, return 0.0.
2. Initialize sum = 0.0.
3. For each car in this.cars:
   a. sum = sum + car.dailyPrice.
4. Return sum / this.cars.size().
Related Features: cars
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: double: The average daily price of all cars, or 0.0 if no cars exist.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned value is the mathematical average of all dailyPrice values.

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: List the customers whose rentals are overdue.
Algorithm:
1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
2. Initialize an empty list 'overdueCustomers'.
3. For each rental in this.rentals:
   a. If rental.backDate is null AND currentDate > rental.dueDate:
      i. If rental.customer is not in 'overdueCustomers', add rental.customer to 'overdueCustomers'.
4. Return 'overdueCustomers'.
Related Features: rentals
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EDate
Input: currentDate (EDate): The current date to compare against due dates.
Output: List<Customer>: A list of customers with overdue rentals, or an empty list.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned list contains customers with at least one rental where backDate is null and dueDate < currentDate.

Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Count how many rental records belong to each customer.
Algorithm:
1. Initialize an empty map 'customerCounts'.
2. For each rental in this.rentals:
   a. If rental.customer exists:
      i. If rental.customer is in 'customerCounts', increment the count.
      ii. Else, add rental.customer to 'customerCounts' with a count of 1.
3. Return 'customerCounts'.
Related Features: rentals
Related Classes: Rental, Customer, EMap
Input: None
Output: EMap: A mapping of customers to the count of their rental records.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned map contains the correct count of rental records for each customer.

  public EList<Car> identifyAvailableCars()
  {
    EList<Car> availableCars = new BasicEList<Car>();
    
    for (Car car : getCars()) {
        boolean isRented = false;
        for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
            if (rental.getCar() == car && rental.getBackDate() == null) {
                isRented = true;
                break;
            }

        if (!isRented) {

  public EList<Customer> findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(final Date currentDate)
  {
    EList<Customer> overdueCustomers = new BasicEList<Customer>();

        return overdueCustomers;
    }
    
    for (Rental rental : getRentals()) {
        if (rental.getBackDate() == null && currentDate.after(rental.getDueDate())) {

            return NAME_EDEFAULT == null ? name != null : !NAME_EDEFAULT.equals(name);
    }
    return super.eIsSet(featureID);
  }

  @Override
  public String toString()
  {
    if (eIsProxy()) return super.toString();
    StringBuilder result = new StringBuilder(super.toString());
    result.append(" (name: ");
    result.append(name);
    result.append(')');
    return result.toString();
  }

} // Store

- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:

2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Counts how many rental records belong to each customer.
Algorithm: 
1. If this.rentals is empty, return an empty map.
2. Initialize an empty map 'counts'.
3. For each rental in this.rentals:
   a. If rental.customer exists in 'counts', increment the value.
   b. Else, add rental.customer to 'counts' with a value of 1.
4. Return 'counts'.
Related Features: rentals
Related Classes: Customer, Rental, EMap
Input: None
Output: EMap (mapping of Customer to Integer count)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned map contains each customer and the number of rental records associated with them.

Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Calculates the total revenue generated by all rental records in the store.
Algorithm: 
1. Initialize 'total' to 0.0.
2. For each rental in this.rentals:
   a. Add rental.totalPrice to 'total'.
3. Return 'total'.
Related Features: rentals
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: double (the sum of all rental total prices)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned value is the sum of all total prices from the rental records.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Computes the average daily price of all cars in the store.
Algorithm: 
1. If this.cars is empty, return 0.0.
2. Initialize 'sum' to 0.0.
3. For each car in this.cars, add car.dailyPrice to 'sum'.
4. Return 'sum' divided by the number of cars.
Related Features: cars
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: double (the average daily price)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the calculated average or 0.0 if no cars exist.

Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Identifies cars currently available in the store, sorted by daily price in ascending order.
Algorithm: 
1. Initialize an empty list 'availableCars'.
2. For each car in this.cars:
   a. Check if there is any rental in this.rentals where rental.car == car and rental.backDate is null.
   b. If no such rental exists, add the car to 'availableCars'.
3. Sort 'availableCars' by dailyPrice in ascending order.
4. Return 'availableCars'.
Related Features: cars, rentals
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car> (a list of available cars sorted by price, or an empty list)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned list contains all cars not tied to an active rental (backDate is null), sorted by daily price.

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Lists customers who have rentals that are overdue.
Algorithm: 
1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
2. Initialize an empty list 'overdueCustomers'.
3. For each rental in this.rentals:
   a. If rental.backDate is null AND currentDate > rental.dueDate:
      i. If rental.customer is not in 'overdueCustomers', add it.
4. Return 'overdueCustomers'.
Related Features: rentals
Related Classes: Customer, Rental, EDate
Input: currentDate (EDate)
Output: List<Customer> (a list of unique customers with overdue rentals, or an empty list)
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned list contains unique customers with at least one rental where backDate is null and the due date has passed.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

  {
    return customer;
  }

  {

  }

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title carrental Model

class Car {
    dailyPrice : double
    model : String
    plate : String
}
class Customer {
    address : String
    name : String
    surname : String
}
class OverdueNotice {
    noticeId : String
    customer : Customer
    sendNoticeTo(customer : Customer) : String
}
class Rental {
    backDate : EDate
    dueDate : EDate
    leasingTerms : String
    rentalDate : EDate
    totalPrice : double
    car : Car
    customer : Customer
}
class Store {
    name : String
    cars : List<Car>
    notices : List<OverdueNotice>
    rentals : List<Rental>
    calculateTotalRevenue() : double
    countCarsRentedPerCustomer() : EMap
    determineAverageDailyPrice() : double
    findCustomersWithOverdueRentals(currentDate : EDate) : List<Customer>
    identifyAvailableCars() : List<Car>
}

OverdueNotice --> Customer : customer
Rental --> Car : car
Rental --> Customer : customer
Store *-- "*" Car : cars
Store *-- "*" OverdueNotice : notices
Store *-- "*" Rental : rentals
@enduml
```

+ Store::calculateTotalRevenue
+ Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer

+ Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals

# Operation Specifications
OverdueNotice::sendNoticeTo
Summary: Sends an overdue notice to a specific customer.
Algorithm: 
  1. Accept a customer object as input.
  2. Generate or retrieve a unique noticeId.
  3. Associate the customer with the notice.
  4. Return a confirmation message or notice reference string.
Related Features: noticeId, customer
Related Classes: Customer, OverdueNotice
Input: customer (Customer): The customer who has a late return.
Output: String: A confirmation message or the notice ID.
Pre-condition: The customer must have an active overdue rental.
Post-condition: A new OverdueNotice record is created and linked to the customer.

# Requirement
```text
Store assistants manage a rented-car gallery for each store. Each store keeps a store name together with the car records, rental records, and overdue-notice records that belong to that location. Every car record stores a plate number, model, and daily price. Customer records store the renter's name, surname, and address. Rental records store the rental date, due date, return date, total price, and leasing terms, and connect the rented car with the customer who rents it. When a customer wants to rent a car, the system shows the cars that are currently available and sorts them by daily price. The system records each rental, calculates total rental revenue, lists overdue customers when the current date passes a due date and the car has not been returned, computes the average daily price of all cars in the store, and counts how many rentals each customer has made. The main office issues overdue notices for late returns, and every overdue notice keeps a notice number so the notice can be tracked for the target customer.
Function Requirement:
1. Identify the cars that are currently available in the store. A car is available when it is not tied to an active rental whose return date is still empty. Return the available cars in ascending order of daily price, or return an empty list when none are available.
2. Calculate the total revenue generated by all rental records stored in the store.
3. List the customers whose rentals are overdue. A rental is overdue when its return date is empty and the current date is later than the due date. Return an empty list when no overdue customers exist, or when the current date input is null.
4. Determine the average daily price of all cars stored in the store. Return 0.0 when the store has no car records.
5. Count how many rental records belong to each customer and return the result as a customer-to-count mapping. Return an empty map when the store has no rental records for any customer.
```

# Operation Specifications
Store::countCarsRentedPerCustomer
Summary: Counts how many rental records belong to each customer.
Algorithm:
  1. If the rentals list is empty, return an empty map.
  2. Initialize a map (customer-to-count).
  3. For each rental in the rentals list:
     a. Increment the count for the associated customer in the map.
  4. Return the map.
Related Features: rentals
Related Classes: Customer, Rental, EMap
Input: None
Output: EMap: A mapping of customers to the number of rentals they have made.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns a map containing the rental counts per customer.

Store::findCustomersWithOverdueRentals
Summary: Lists customers whose rentals are overdue.
Algorithm:
  1. If currentDate is null, return an empty list.
  2. Initialize an empty list of customers.
  3. For each rental in the rentals list:
     a. If the rental's return date (backDate) is empty and the currentDate is later than the dueDate:
        i. Add the rental's customer to the list if not already present.
  4. Return the list of customers.
Related Features: rentals
Related Classes: Customer, Rental, EDate
Input: currentDate (EDate): The current system date.
Output: List<Customer>: A list of customers with overdue rentals, or an empty list if none exist or input is null.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns a list of unique customers who have at least one overdue rental.

Store::identifyAvailableCars
Summary: Identifies cars currently available in the store, sorted by daily price.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize an empty list of available cars.
  2. For each car in the cars list:
     a. Check if there is any rental associated with this car where the return date (backDate) is empty.
     b. If no such rental exists, add the car to the available list.
  3. Sort the available list by dailyPrice in ascending order.
  4. Return the sorted list.
Related Features: cars, rentals
Related Classes: Car, Rental
Input: None
Output: List<Car>: A list of available cars sorted by daily price, or an empty list if none are available.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: The returned list contains only cars that are not currently out on rental.

Store::calculateTotalRevenue
Summary: Calculates the total revenue generated by all rental records in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. Initialize totalRevenue to 0.0.
  2. For each rental in the rentals list:
     a. Add the rental's totalPrice to totalRevenue.
  3. Return totalRevenue.
Related Features: rentals
Related Classes: Rental
Input: None
Output: double: The sum of all rental total prices.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the cumulative revenue from all rental records.

Store::determineAverageDailyPrice
Summary: Computes the average daily price of all cars in the store.
Algorithm:
  1. If the cars list is empty, return 0.0.
  2. Calculate the sum of dailyPrice for all cars in the cars list.
  3. Divide the sum by the number of cars.
  4. Return the result.
Related Features: cars
Related Classes: Car
Input: None
Output: double: The average daily price of all cars, or 0.0 if the store has no cars.
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: Returns the calculated average daily price.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

    }

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information

  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.
